R: === shree-tutor-api.md
---
name: shree-tutor-api
description: "Shree's composer DLLs answer \"what does this key type\" directly — the exact call shapes that work"
metadata: 
  node_type: memory
  type: reference
  originSessionId: 69d8b2a2-ed7d-447f-9ed1-ba585383f6f5
  modified: 2026-09-27T05:07:17.814Z
---

Shree-Lipi's per-script composer DLLs (`C:\Shree-Lipi nxt\S8<Script><Family>64.dll`,
e.g. `S8GujU64.dll`) export a Tutor API that answers, key by key, what a keyboard
types. **Load standalone** — no SLNXT, no hook, no window — so it runs in the
background while the machine is in use.

Families: `U` = Unicode, `X` = Ex fonts, `7` = Shree-Lipi 7 fonts (its export drops
the W: `TutorGetMappedCode`), `G` = ?. There are `San` (Sanskrit) and `Dia`
(Diacritical) composers too; Sanskrit answers exactly as Devanagari does.

**Call shapes that work** (all stdcall; give every other argument its own zeroed
4096-byte page — a null crashes the process):

- `TutorGetKbdName(byte[] buf, int len)` → writes e.g. "Modular.Guj". **It lies**:
  it reports that name whatever is loaded, and the table a composer starts with is
  InScript (49/50 against the published Gujarati InScript chart), not Modular.
  Score against the charts instead.
- `TutorGetMappedCodeW(a1..a8)`: **a1 = the ASCII key char**, **a6 = the layer
  (0 = printed keyboard, 1 = the Ctrl+Space third layer)**, **a7 = out buffer
  (UTF-16)**, return = unit count. Getting a6 wrong corrupts every answer — that
  is what made an ear

## 0. What this copy is based on (read this first)

### The rebase

Round 1 was built on `scratchpad\snap-base`. Main had moved on a lot since then:
- the engine's undo stack, the Ctrl+Space fix and the legacy table for unhandled keys;
- the shared non-Indic policy;
- the Bangla merge.

So at the start of round 2 I re-based the copy onto **a snapshot of main taken at 19:41**. The snapshot is in `scratchpad\r3\tbl-pun\round2\main-base`: 890 files (src, tests, tools, assets, FontSoftware.sln, logo.png), no bin or obj. Its rebase log is `round2\rebase.log`.

- **Every file not listed in sections 1 to 3 is byte-identical to main@19:41.** For those files, keep main's version.
- **Two files were taken from main later, at 20:07 and 20:13** (see below).
- **Base for a three-way merge:** use `round2\main-base`. Using `snap-base` also works, because every Punjabi edit below is re-applied by a script or is a self-contained file.

/// <summary>
/// The regularities of Shree-Lipi 7 Gurmukhi as rewrite rules on the Unicode text, so a syllable the composer
/// was never asked about is still built the way it builds one (every rule is read off pun7-groundtruth.json):
/// the i-sign first, the spacer BE after ਠ and ਨ with the marks that sit on top of the letter before it and
/// ੁ ੂ after it as AE AF, the far addak A8 after a mark that reaches the top, the bindi A4 after ਉ ਊ, a tippi
/// struck after a vowel sign written as bindi. The rules work on Unicode (never on the legacy codes), so ASCII
/// passing through - English text next to Gurmukhi - is never taken for a sign. Places the rules decide are
/// marked with noncharacters, which the pairs turn into codes. The learner then needs pairs only for the true
/// exceptions.

/// <summary>
/// Learns the Shree-Pun -> Unicode pairs of one family: every preferred composer spelling must read back to its
/// Unicode, or to a Unicode string the table writes with the very same bytes.
///
/// The pre rules undo what the composer does to the order around ਠ and ਨ: the marks it writes before the spacer BE
/// go back after the letter's ੁ ੂ and subjoined form, and BE (and the blank BF) go. The pairs answer in visual
/// order (the i-sign in front of its cluster, as the bytes are), and the post rules move it to its Unicode place,
/// join ੲ ਅ ੳ with a sign into the vowel the Unicode composer writes for them (ਇ ਈ ਏ ਆ ਐ ਔ ਉ ਊ), ੱ + ਂ into ਁ
/// (as the Unicode composer writes it), and keep a visible halant before ਰ ਹ ਵ ਯ apart with a ZWNJ (ਕ੍‌ਰ, 48 B5 63
/// - plain ਕ੍ਰ is the subjoined form 6E).
/// </summary>
public sealed class Reader
{

    public readonly List<RewriteRule> PreRules;
    public readonly List<RewriteRule> PostRules;
    readonly FontConverter _stager, _visual;

    /// <summary>Marks the virama of the visible halant B5 (a noncharacter): before ਰ ਹ ਵ ਯ it becomes a ZWNJ.</summary>
    public const string HalantMark = "\uFDDD";

    /// <summary>Holds an i-sign in place while the i-sign rule moves the others (a noncharacter).</summary>
    public const string HeldISign = "\uFDDE";

    /// <summary>The marks th

public static class Checks
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Every truth string alone: preferred, another accepted spelling, or wrong. A string whose spelling passes a
    /// character through (₹ in a 4/5/6 font: "U+20B9") must come out so, reporting exactly that character.
    /// </summary>
    public static string Truth(FontConverter conv, IReadOnlyList<Truth> truth, out List<string> fails)
    {
        int pref = 0, acc = 0;
        fails = new List<string>();
        foreach (var t in truth)
        {
            var r = conv.Convert(t.U);
            string got = Hex(r.Text);
            if (!ReportsAsSpelled(r, t.Pref)) fails.Add($"{t.U}\t[{Cps(t.U)}]\tunmapped {string.Join("", r.UnmappedCharacters)}\tgot {got}");
            else if (got == t.Pref) pref++;
            else if (t.Acc.Contains(got)) acc++;
            else fails.Add($"{t.U}\t[{Cps(t.U)}]\twant {t.Pref}{(t.Acc.Length > 1 ? " (or " + string.Join(" | ", t.Acc.Where(a => a != t.Pref)) + ")" : "")}\tgot {got}");
        }
        return $"truth {truth.Count}: preferred {pref}, other accepted {acc}, wrong {fails.Count} ({100.0 * pref / truth.Count:F2}% preferred)";
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// Truth strings that can stand next to each other in a word: they start on a base (consonant, vowel, digit,
    /// punctuation), do not end on a virama or joiner (which would join the next consonant) and have no sign straight
    /// after a virama.
    /// </summ

// PunTruth - build and check the Shree-Lipi Punjabi (Gurmukhi) tables against what Shree's own composers write
// (tests/FontSoftware.Tests/Data/shree-pun7-truth.json: 27,752 Unicode strings and the bytes S8Pun764 types for each).
//
// The tables are rules plus pairs. The rules (Rules.cs) carry what the composer does everywhere: the i-sign before
// its cluster, the spacer BE after ਠ ਨ with the top marks before it and ੁ ੂ after it as AE AF, the far addak A8
// after a mark that reaches the top, the bindi A4 after ਉ ਊ, a tippi after a vowel sign written as the bindi. The
// learner (Learner.cs) then adds a pair only where composing from smaller pieces gives the wrong bytes, so a syllable
// outside the truth is still built the composer's way. One table pair per font family (Family.cs: the 7 faces and the
// 4/5/6 faces are the same design but for ₹ 3C).
//
// Text that is not Gurmukhi follows tools/Shared/ShreeNonIndic.cs (and Ascii.cs for ASCII): punctuation the fonts draw
// is written as its look-alike, any other character whose code the fonts map is reported as unmapped and left out.
//
//   puntruth prep                          the capture: rows, codes written, accepted spellings
//   puntruth fixture                       write the test fixture from the capture
//   puntruth build [outDir] [fam...]       learn and check every family's tables, write them (default assets/config/mappings,
//

/// <summary>
/// A virama that ends a word. Shree's composer is asked one key at a time, so a string
/// that ends in a bare virama (C + virama, kind canonical:pending-link) is answered with
/// the half form the composer shows while it WAITS for the next letter - and a half letter
/// standing alone draws a broken letter. Unicode text that ends a word in a virama is drawn
/// by every Unicode font with a visible halant, so the table writes what the composer
/// itself writes for C + virama + ZWNJ (the visible halant). The rows keep the composer's
/// pending answer; this says what the table writes instead, and why.
/// </summary>
public static class WordFinal
{
    public const char Zwnj = '‌';

    /// <summary>
    /// For a truth row that ends in letter + virama: the halant spelling and how it was
    /// derived from the truth; null for any other row, or when the composer's own answer
    /// already is the halant spelling (the letters with no half form).
    /// <list type="number">
    /// <item>The truth has the same string + ZWNJ (every single letter, and the ligature
    ///   letters क्ष ज्ञ त्त त्र श्र): the composer's spelling of that.</item>
    /// <item>A cluster whose spelling ends in its last letter's own spelling (स्त = half स +
    ///   त): the earlier letters as the composer writes them in the cluster, the last letter

R: // node render.js <out.png> <font file> "<label>=<hex bytes>" ... : draw byte strings in a legacy font by glyph index
// (bytes with no glyph are drawn as nothing and listed). Default font C:/Windows/Fonts/WTEL7001.TTF.
const { load, sheet } = require('./ttf');
const [out, fontFile, ...items] = process.argv.slice(2);
const f = load(fontFile || 'C:/Windows/Fonts/WTEL7001.TTF');
const rows = items.map(it => {
  const [label, hex] = it.includes('=') ? it.split('=') : [it, it];
  const bytes = hex.trim().split(/\s+/).map(h => parseInt(h, 16));
  const missing = bytes.filter(b => !f.bytes.has(b));
  if (missing.length) console.log(label, 'no glyph for', missing.map(b => b.toString(16)).join(' '));
  return { label: label.replace(/[^0-9A-F +\-|]/gi, '').toUpperCase().slice(0, 40), runs: [{ font: f, glyphs: bytes.filter(b => f.bytes.has(b)).map(b => f.bytes.get(b).glyph) }] };
});
sheet(out, rows, { px: 64, cellW: 330, cols: 3 });
console.log('wrote', out, rows.length);

// --by-series: register by what the font IS, then prove it.
//
// Grouping by the exact set of code points a font maps splits one encoding into
// dozens of groups, because designs of the same series carry different glyph
// subsets — SHREE-GUJX-5227 was treated as its own layout although the shree-gujx
// table renders it correctly. Shree's own taxonomy is in the family name:
// SHREE-<SCRIPT><SERIES>-<design>, where the series is X, 7 or nothing.
//
// Naming alone is not proof, so each candidate is checked against the table it
// would use: every legacy code that table can emit must exist in the font's cmap.
if (args.Contains("--by-series"))
{
    var seriesToEnc = new Dictionary

/// <summary>
/// Text that is not Indic in the Shree 7 and 4/5/6 tables. These are 8-bit fonts: every
/// code of the Windows-1252 range draws a glyph of the script (“ is 93 - ન in SHREE-GUJ7, the
/// conjunct ઙ્ગ in SHREE-GUJ - and © is A9, the vowel sign ૃ), so a character with no pair,
/// passed through as itself, printed a letter. One policy for every table:
/// punctuation the fonts can show is written as the look-alike they draw (“ ” as two single
/// quotes, … as three periods, • as the font's bullet B7, ‚ „ as commas); any other
/// character whose code the fonts map is reported as unmapped and left out; everything
/// else passes through and is reported (the fonts have no glyph for it, so it is drawn in
/// another font, as itself). ASCII passes through every table by design.
/// </summary>
public class ShreeNonIndicTests
{

    };

TOOL Bash: {"command":"R=\"C:/Users/harde/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-harde-Desktop-Windows-Font-Software/7673923b-96b4-4287-87dd-a8e7c6533eca/scratchpad/r3/tbl-tel/repo\"; sed -n 80,200p \"$R/src/FontSoftware.Core/Conversion/FontConverter.cs\"","description":"Read remainder of FontConverter"}
R:             if (match is not null)
            {
                sb.Append(match.To);
                // A pair for a character the target cannot show: reported all the same.
                if (match.Unmapped)
                    unmapped.Add(match.From);
                i += match.From.Length;
            }
            else if (staged[i] is >= '\uFE00' and <= '\uFE0F')
            {
                // A variation selector picks one shape of what comes before it (the new
                // ruu, U+E3C6, against the old one Shree fonts draw for plain રૂ). A table
                // that has no entry for it has one shape only: it is dropped, never
                // passed into a legacy font as a character of its own.
                i++;
            }
            else
            {
                // No mapping: pass the character through and record it (FR-4).
                // Respect surrogate pairs so emoji/rare glyphs aren't split.
                int len = char.IsHighSurrogate(staged[i]) && i + 1 < staged.Length ? 2 : 1;
                string ch = staged.Substring(i, len);
                sb.Append(ch);
                if (!char.IsWhiteSpace(staged[i]))
                    unmapped.Add(ch);
                i += len;
            }
        }

        string result = ApplyRules(_postRules, sb.ToString());
        return new ConversionResult
        {
            Text = result,
            UnmappedCharacters = unmapped.ToArray()
        };
    }

Places the rules decide are marked with noncharacters, which the pairs turn into codes. /// <summary>Hold-out: learn on all folds but one, convert the strings left out.</summary>
public static class CrossCheck
{
    /// <summary>
    /// The units text is built from are always known: single characters, a letter with a sign or a tail (the
    /// letter-specific shapes, gunintham), a letter with the visible halant (its halant form), క్ష alone, with a sign or
    /// with the halant (one letter), and the ligature శ్రీ. Everything longer - clusters, the ra-vattu, signs and tails on
    /// clusters - is held out in turn.
    /// </summary>
    public static bool Unit(string u) => u.Length <= 2 || Shape(u) is "CHN" || u == "శ్రీ" ||
        (u.StartsWith("క్ష", StringComparison.Ordinal) && (u.Length == 3 || (u.Length == 4 && IsVowelSign(u[3])) || u == "క్ష్‌"));

        int ok = 0, n = 0;

The rules (Rules.cs) carry what the composer does everywhere: a sign on a cluster on its\n// first letter with the vattu after it, ృ after the vattu behind the spacer A0, ై as the e-shape + 4F (95 before a wide\n// vattu), the ra-vattu hook before its cluster (5B with ై; the combined 88 89 after స ష), క్ష as one letter with its vattu\n// behind a spacer, the visible halant for a virama with no consonant after it. The learner (Learner.cs) then adds a pair\n// only where composing from smaller pieces gives the wrong bytes, so a syllable outside the truth is still built the\n// composer's way. One table pair per font family (Family.cs: the 7 and 4/5/6 Apoorva/Asmitha faces are one design; the\n// Shree-Tel7-001/002 faces draw a star at 92 where the others draw ₹).\n//\n// Text that is not TOOL Write: {"file_path":"C:\\Users\\harde\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-harde-Desktop-Windows-Font-Software\\7673923b-96b4-4287-87dd-a8e7c6533eca\\scratchpad\\r3\\tbl-tel\\round1\\js\\patch-rev-learn.txt","content":"    public Dictionary<string, string> Learn(IReadOnlyList<Truth> truth, FontConverter forward, IEnumerable<char> fontCodes, out string stats, List<string>? log = null)\n    {\n        var canon = Canonical(truth);\n        var pairs = new Dictionary<string, string>(StringComparer.Ordinal);\n        foreach (var (hex, u) in FontCodes) pairs[Staged(FromHex(hex))] = u;\n        foreach (var t in truth.Where(t => t.U.Length == 1 && t.Pref.Length > 0 && Reported(t.Pref).Count == 0))\n            if (canon[t.Pref] == t.U) pairs.TryAdd(Staged(FromHex(t.Pref)), Visual(t.U));\n        int atoms = pairs.Count, added = 0, unkeyable = 0;\n\n        foreach (var level in canon.GroupBy(kv => Staged(FromHex(kv.Key)).Length).OrderBy(g => g.Key))\n        {\n            var conv = new FontConverter(Build(pairs));\n            foreach (var (hex, u) in level.OrderBy(kv => kv.Key, StringComparer.Ordinal))\n            {\n                string got = conv.ConvertText(FromHex(hex));\n                if (Same(got, u, hex, forward)) continue;\n                string key = Staged(FromHex(hex));\n                if (key.Length == 0) { unkeyable++; continue; }\n                log?.Add($\"{u}\\t{hex}\\t{Hex(key)}\\tread {got}\");\n                pairs[key] = Visual(u);\n                added++;\n            }\n        }\n        int pieces = 0;\n        foreach (var (code, u) in Pieces(truth, fontCodes))\n            if (pairs.TryAdd(Staged(code.ToString()), u)) pieces++;\n        stats = $\"{F.Id} reverse: atoms {atoms}, learned {added}, unkeyable {unkeyable}, pieces read alone {pieces}, total {pairs.Count}\";\n        return pairs;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Every code of the fonts read alone, so no code ever passes through as the Latin letter of its code point (a piece\n    /// of a glyph the composer wrote on its own: its carrier E6 61 E6 61 for a lone sign, the e-shape F2 of its lone ై key,\n    /// the pieces of ధ it writes for ౢ). Read off the truth: a code that begins the spelling of a letter alone (ధ = AB A7 E6 FE)\n    /// reads as that letter; a code a sign adds to a letter's spelling (కా = 4D 3E: 3E) reads as that sign; any other code\n    /// of a letter's spelling (the talakattu E6, the spacers FC-FF) as nothing. The most frequent reading wins.\n    /// </summary>\n    public static List<(char Code, string Unicode)> Pieces(IReadOnlyList<Truth> truth, IEnumerable<char> fontCodes)\n    {\n        var byU = truth.ToDictionary(t => t.U, StringComparer.Ordinal);\n        var votes = new Dictionary<char, Dictionary<string, int>>();\n        void Vote(char c, string u) { if (!votes.TryGetValue(c, out var d)) votes[c] = d = new(); d[u] = d.GetValueOrDefault(u) + 1; }\n        foreach (var t in truth.Where(t => t.Composer is null && t.Pref.Length > 0 && Reported(t.Pref).Count == 0))\n        {\n            var codes = FromHex(t.Pref);\n            if (t.U.Length == 1 && (IsCons(t.U[0]) || IsVowel(t.U[0])))\n                for (int i = 0; i < codes.Length; i++) Vote(codes[i], i == 0 ? t.U : \"\");\n            else if (t.U.Length == 2 && IsCons(t.U[0]) && IsVowelSign(t.U[1]) && byU.TryGetValue(t.U[..1], out var letter))\n            {\n                var own = FromHex(letter.Pref);\n                foreach (char c in codes.Where(c => !own.Contains(c))) Vote(c, t.U[1..]);\n            }\n        }\n        var list = new List<(char, string)>();\n        foreach (char c in fontCodes.Where(c => c > ' '))\n            list.Add((c, votes.TryGetValue(c, out var d) ? d.OrderByDescending(kv => kv.Value).ThenBy(kv => kv.Key, StringComparer.Ordinal).First().Key : \"\"));\n        return list;\n    }\n"}

/// <summary>
/// The Shree-Lipi Punjabi (Gurmukhi) tables against what Shree's own composers write. The fixture
/// (Data/shree-pun7-truth.json) holds 27,752 Unicode strings - every letter with every sign and tail (bindi, tippi,
/// addak, visarga), ਿ first, the subjoined ੍ਰ ੍ਹ ੍ਵ ੍ਯ, the nukta letters, every two-letter cluster through the halant,
/// the independent vowels and carriers - with the bytes S8Pun764 (Shree-Lipi 7) types for each and the other
/// spellings it also accepts. Nothing here needs Shree installed.
///
/// The 7 faces (SHREE-PUN7-0951/0952) and the 4/5/6 faces (SHREE-PUN-0950/0951/0952/0955/0956) are the same design
/// code for code, but ₹ (3C) is in the 7 faces only: the shree-pun456 tables pass ₹ through, reported.
/// tools/PunTruth builds both table pairs from the capture.
/// </summary>
public class ShreePunjabiTruthTests
{
    private sealed record Row(string U, string Pref, string[] Accepted);

    private static readonly Lazy<List<Row>> Truth = new(LoadTruth);

    // ₹: 3C in the 7 faces; the 4/5/6 faces have no glyph there, so ₹ passes through, reported. Both read 3C as ₹.
    [Fact]
    public void Rupee()
    {
        var seven = To("shree-pun7").Convert("₹੧੦੦");
        Assert.Equal("3C 31 30 30", Hex(seven.Text));
        Assert.Empty(seven.UnmappedCharacters);
        var four = To("shree-pun456").Convert("₹੧੦੦");
        Assert.Equal("U+20B9 31 30 30", Hex(four.Text));
        Assert.Contains("₹", four.UnmappedCharacters);
        Assert.Equal("₹੧੦੦", From("shree-pun7").ConvertText(FromHex("3C 31 30 30")));
        Assert.Equal("₹੧੦੦", From("shree-pun456").ConvertText(FromHex("3C 31 30 30")));
    }

    // (f) Text that is not Gurmukhi (P2-P4): what the fonts draw as itself is written as itself, unreported (the digits

using System.Text;
using FontSoftware.Core.Models;

namespace FontSoftware.Core.Keyboards;

/// <summary>
/// The order a keyboard is typed in, read off the keyboard's own runs.
///
/// A vendor chart shows how a sign goes onto a letter once, on ka. On the ITR
/// Gujarati keyboard "rf" is કિ - the i-sign struck first, the way a typewriter
/// strikes it - and "fo" is ર્ક, the reph struck after its letter. The layouts
/// carry exactly those runs, so ka was the only letter that came out right: r then
/// h typed િર where the keyboard means રિ, and m then the reph typed મર્ for ર્મ.
/// What a chart shows on ka is how the keyboard treats every consonant, so it is
/// learned here from those runs and applied to all of them as the word is typed:
///
/// - a sign struck before its letter goes after that letter's whole cluster once
///   the cluster is complete (િ, then સ્, then થ is સ્થિ);
/// - the reph struck after its letter goes in front of that letter's cluster;
/// - a key that types a vowel on its own types the vowel's sign straight after a
///   consonant, as Newroma's "e" is એ but "ke" is કે - so "re" is રે, not રએ;
/// - two signs a run puts together into one (ા then ે is ો) become that one.
///
/// A keyboard whose runs teach none of this - InScript, typed in Unicode's ow

    /// <summary>
    /// What a key's text is in the typed word: a sign struck before its letter, or
    /// a reph struck after it, stands in for itself until its cluster is known.
    /// The stand-in is as long as the text, so a run that later replaces it can
    /// count on that.
    /// </summary>
    public string Mark(string output)
    {
        int pre = _preSigns.IndexOf(output);
        if (pre >= 0) return ((char)(FirstPreMark + pre)).ToString();
        if (_reph is not null && output == _reph) return _reph[0].ToString() + RephMark;
        return output;
    }

R: using System.Text;
using FontSoftware.Core.Models;

namespace FontSoftware.Core.Conversion;

/// <summary>
/// Phonetic "type by sound" converter. Turns a roman string into Indic script
/// using a <see cref="TransliterationScheme"/> — e.g. "namaste" → नमस्ते,
/// "gujaraatii" → ગુજરાતી. Deterministic and rule-based (not dictionary-based),
/// so words are spelled the way they sound.
///
/// Composition rules:
///  - A consonant carries an inherent 'a'. Two consonants in a row form a conjunct,
///    so a virama is inserted between them.
///  - A vowel after a consonant becomes a matra (the inherent 'a' uses an empty
///    matra, i.e. it just leaves the bare consonant).
///  - A vowel not preceded by a consonant becomes its independent form.
///  - A word-final consonant keeps its inherent 'a' (no trailing virama).
/// </summary>
public sealed class Transliterator
{
    private enum Kind { Consonant, Vowel, Special }

    private readonly TransliterationScheme _scheme;
    // All roman tokens, longest first, so "kh"/"aa" win over "k"/"a".
    private readonly List<(string Roman, Kind Kind)> _tokens;

    public Transliterator(TransliterationScheme scheme)
    {
        _scheme = scheme ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(scheme));

        _tokens = new List<(string, Kind)>();
        foreach (var k in scheme.Consonants.Keys) _tokens.Add((k, Kind.Consonant));
        foreach (var k in scheme.Vowels.Keys) _tokens.Add((k, Kind.Vowel));
        foreach (var k in scheme.Sp

using FontSoftware.Core.Conversion;
using FontSoftware.Core.Models;
using static Common;

/// <summary>
/// Learns the Shree-Tel -> Unicode pairs of one family: every preferred spelling must read back to its Unicode, or to a
/// Unicode string the table writes with the very same bytes - and so must every other spelling Shree's composer wrote
/// (the accepted ones, the alternatives, the vendor keys, the quirks: Capture.Spellings), as text, never Latin.
///
/// The pre rules drop the spacers the composer puts before a vattu (A0 or 9B after క్ష, A0 before ృ). The pairs answer
/// in visual order - a sign on a cluster right after its first letter, as the bytes are, the ra-vattu hook as a mark
/// before its cluster - and the post rules put Unicode order back: the sign after the subjoined letters, ె + \u0C56 = ై, the
/// hook as ్ర after the last consonant of the cluster it stands before (or, with no cluster after it - the composer's
/// pending hook - of the one before it), and a visible halant (్ + ZWNJ) that ends a word as the virama alone.
/// </summary>
public sealed class Reader
{
    public readonly Fam F;
    public readonly List<RewriteRule> PreRules;
    public readonly List<RewriteRule> PostRules;
    readonly FontConverter _stager, _visual;

    /// <summary>The ra-vattu hook in visual order (a noncharacter): 7B, 5B, 93.</summary>
    public const string Hook = Rules.Ra;

    /// <summary>The vattu codes (and the combined 87 88 8

R: shree-tel7-to-unicode.json
unicode-to-shree-tel7.json
5:// TelTruth - build and check the Shree-Lipi Telugu tables against what Shree's own composers write
6:// (tests/FontSoftware.Tests/Data/shree-tel7-truth.json: the Unicode strings and the bytes S8Tel764 types for each).
7://
8:// The tables are rules plus pairs. The rules (Rules.cs) carry what the composer does everywhere: a sign on a cluster on its
9:// first letter with the vattu after it, ృ after the vattu behind the spacer A0, ై as the e-shape + 4F (95 before a wide
10:// vattu), the ra-vattu hook before its cluster (5B with ై; the combined 88 89 after స ష), క్ష as one letter with its vattu
11:// behind a spacer, the visible halant for a virama with no consonant after it. The learner (Learner.cs) then adds a pair
12:// only where composing from smaller pieces gives the wrong bytes, so a syllable outside the truth is still built the
13:// composer's way. One table pair per font family (Family.cs: the 7 and 4/5/6 Apoorva/Asmitha faces are one design; the
14:// Shree-Tel7-001/002 faces draw a star at 92 where the others draw ₹).
15://
16:// Text that is not Telugu follows tools/Shared/ShreeNonIndic.cs (and Ascii.cs for ASCII): punctuation the fonts draw is
17:// written as its look-alike, any other character whose code the fonts map is reported as unmapped and left out, a
18:// character no code draws passes through as itself, reported.
19://
20://   teltruth prep                          the capture: rows, codes written

    public static void WriteLimits(string path, Dictionary<string, List<string>> byFamily)
    {
        var doc = JsonNode.Parse(File.ReadAllText(path))!.AsObject();
        foreach (var (id, list) in byFamily) doc[id] = new JsonArray(list.Select(x => (JsonNode)x).ToArray());
        File.WriteAllText(path, doc.ToJsonString(Writer));
        Console.WriteLine($"wrote {string.Join(", ", byFamily.Keys)} into {path}");
    }
}

R:     /// Characters a reader could type that the table leaves unmapped (it passes them through or leaves them out, and
    /// the converter reports them): punctuation and symbols of ASCII and beyond, and the Telugu block. This is the
    /// family's line in assets/config/encoding-limits.json.
    /// </summary>
    public static List<string> Limits(FontConverter fwd)
    {
        var probe = Enumerable.Range(0x21, 0x5E).Select(c => (char)c).Where(c => !char.IsLetterOrDigit(c))
            .Concat("“”‘’–—…•×÷₹°॥।").Concat(Enumerable.Range(0x0C00, 0x80).Select(c => (char)c)
                .Where(c => char.GetUnicodeCategory(c) != System.Globalization.UnicodeCategory.OtherNotAssigned));
        return probe.Where(c => fwd.Convert(c.ToString()).HasUnmapped).Select(c => ((int)c).ToString("X4")).Distinct().OrderBy(x => x).ToList();
    }

    public static void WriteLimits(string path, Dictionary<string, List<string>> byFamily)
    {
        var doc = JsonNode.Parse(File.ReadAllText(path))!.AsObject();
        foreach (var (id, list) in byFamily) doc[id] = new JsonArray(list.Select(x => (JsonNode)x).ToArray());
        File.WriteAllText(path, doc.ToJsonString(Writer));
        Console.WriteLine($"wrote {string.Join(", ", byFamily.Keys)} into {path}");
    }
